Top Songs of 2023

The top songs of 2023 captured a defining moment in music where genre lines blurred, global sounds flourished, and viral discovery drove chart success. With pop, hip-hop, R&B, and Latin music all sharing the spotlight, 2023 showcased the power of streaming and social media to elevate both breakout stars and established icons. From introspective ballads to dance-ready anthems, this year’s biggest songs reflected a bold, borderless music industry.

Whether you’re revisiting the 2023 top songs or discovering the year’s biggest hits for the first time, this archive features the complete weekly Top 40 rankings from one of the most dynamic years in modern music.

How We Ranked the Top Songs of 2023

*Note: Based on the Nolan Method, these are the year-end best songs of 2023 that represented the Top 40 songs during the calendar year 2023. The Nolan Method ranking system was formulated by lead Top40Weekly contributor Jarrett Nolan and is based on a point system for an artist’s chart performance on Billboard charts.

Notable Songs & Artists from 2023

With genre-bending hits, global dominance from Latin and Afrobeats artists, and a digital-first music culture, the top songs of 2023 showcased an era where creativity, diversity, and connectivity drive the future of the industry.

Taylor Swift & Miley Cyrus Lead a Pop Powerhouse Year

Pop reigned supreme in 2023, with Taylor Swift and Miley Cyrus dominating global charts. Taylor’s introspective single “Anti-Hero” became a streaming juggernaut, thanks to its honest lyrics and fan-favorite status during her Eras Tour. Meanwhile, Miley’s empowering breakup anthem “Flowers” topped global charts for weeks, embracing self-love and independence with a disco-tinged groove that resonated across generations.

Dua Lipa, Olivia Rodrigo, and Harry Styles also delivered major pop moments, ensuring that catchy hooks, personal storytelling, and cinematic visuals defined the year’s mainstream sound.

Hip-Hop & R&B Define the Charts with Emotion & Innovation

Drake, SZA, and The Weeknd carried hip-hop and R&B to new heights in 2023. SZA’s “Kill Bill” became a dark R&B-pop hit, pairing a sultry vocal delivery with twisted love-story lyrics that captivated listeners. Drake’s continued partnership with 21 Savage delivered chart-topping tracks that combined trap production with signature swagger, while The Weeknd extended his 1980s revival wave with the hauntingly retro “Die for You”, remixed with Ariana Grande for even wider reach.

Latin Music & Afrobeats Break New Ground Globally

Latin music’s global momentum was unstoppable in 2023. Bad Bunny returned with more hits from Un Verano Sin Ti, while Peso Pluma became a breakout star with “Ella Baila Sola”, introducing regional Mexican music to a broader international audience. Karol G also had a landmark year, with “TQG” (featuring Shakira) topping charts across Latin America and the U.S.

Afrobeats continued its ascent, with Rema’s “Calm Down” (remixed with Selena Gomez) and Burna Boy’s festival-ready singles introducing African rhythms to mainstream pop playlists worldwide.

Viral Trends & the TikTok Effect

Social media—especially TikTok—continued to shape music discovery, turning unexpected releases into viral chart-toppers. Ice Spice burst onto the scene with “Boy’s a Liar Pt. 2” (with PinkPantheress), a lo-fi hit that became one of the year’s most recognizable earworms. Steve Lacy’s “Bad Habit” extended its viral success into the new year, while Metro Boomin’s cinematic soundscapes powered hits like “Creepin’” and “Superhero”.

This viral success confirmed that fan engagement, remixes, and creator culture are now integral to chart performance.
